# WPT Conformance Progress — Campaign Ledger

Tracks the Web Platform Tests we've worked on and their latest pass/total ratios,
measured via `scripts/wpt_run.py` over CDP against a `--features render` server.

**How to reproduce a row:**
```sh
cargo build --release --features render
./target/release/obscura serve --port 9222 --render-mode on-demand --stealth &
.venv/bin/python scripts/wpt_run.py <test-path> --base https://wpt.live
```

> 🧭 **STANDING ORDER (2026-08-02): prioritise the UNTOUCHED realms over deepening the ones we already hold.**
> The frontier survey found we cover **21 of WPT's 262 top-level realms** — inside them we score 88–99%,
> outside them **0–48%** against a Chrome at 93–100%, and the outside ones are the realms a page needs to
> *function*: `fetch` 15.5%, `cookies` 0.8%, `accname` **0.0%**. Open quests **F1–F7** at the top of
> [`tickets/00-THE-QUEST-BOARD.md`](tickets/00-THE-QUEST-BOARD.md); measured map in
> [`tickets/102-the-frontier-survey.md`](tickets/102-the-frontier-survey.md). Ledger rows below are the
> realms already held — a ⭐ inside one of them is a bankable follow-up, not the next move.

Branch: `engine-per-page-threads`. Last updated: 2026-08-04 (Quests #460–#462 — **the streaming arc**: *a stream is how the platform says "here is data, but not all of it, and not yet."* Obscura's answer was twenty-five lines, and its `tee()` returned two **empty** streams. **`streams` 181/1211 (14.9%) → 1390/1474 (94.3%), 61 of 71 files to 100%, ZERO regressions** — and excluding `streams/transferable/`, the one band that needs a capability we do not have, **we score 1390/1398 against a Chrome at 1344/1398 on the identical files.** **The baseline's shape mattered more than its size:** `templated` 48/91, `pipe-through` 9/43 — *not zeros*. A realm that scores 15% and never zero is not **missing**, it is **approximated**, and an approximation of a stream is worse than an absence: `tee() { return [new ReadableStream(), new ReadableStream()]; }` does not fail loudly, it hands back two empty streams, and a page that tees a response to cache one copy and render the other renders nothing with no error anywhere. **#460 the readable side** (87/375 → **367/371**): a stream is a QUEUE with a high-water mark, not a buffer; a reader's LOCK is the only thing between a caller and silently interleaved data; and `tee()`'s hard part is not duplication but INDEPENDENCE. Three bugs worth keeping: **`null` is an EMPTY DICTIONARY, not "not an object"** — WebIDL converts `null` and `undefined` alike and the whole difference is `typeof null === 'object'`, so an ordinary `isObject` guard *refused* `new ReadableStream()`, 25 rows on the most ordinary call in the API; **half-fixing it changed nothing**, because `null` then flowed straight into a property read one line later, and the byte-identical re-measure is exactly what a half-fix looks like. **`releaseLock()` must REJECT every outstanding read** — the chunk that read was waiting for now belongs to whoever takes the lock next, so an `await reader.read()` never returns; 54 rows sat as `notrun` behind that one hang. And `return()` joins the same queue `next()` does. `from.any.html` **49/50 against a Chrome at 14/50**; `async-iterator` 41/41 against 40/41. **#461 the writable side and the pipe** (68/549 → **546/549**; Chrome 536/549): a writable stream's whole job is to say *"not yet"*, and `pipeTo` is a state machine, not a loop — source errors → destination aborted, destination errors → source cancelled, signal aborts → both. `WritableStreamAbort` must **re-read** the state after signalling, because the signal's listeners run synchronously and one may abort again. **🔍 And the finding that is not a streams bug at all: `setTimeout(fn, 0)` was a MICROTASK.** HTML runs one task, then drains the **entire** microtask queue, then the next task; ours interleaved. Measured directly: **three of five hundred** chained promise jobs had run by the time a 0ms timer fired. Every idiom meaning *"let everything pending settle, then look"* — `await delay(0)`, WPT's own `flushAsyncEvents`, a render scheduled after a data update — was looking at a half-finished world. Fixed by pumping real tasks through `op_sleep(0)`, one at a time in insertion order; **500/500** after. The proof is the strongest kind: with *only that hunk* reverted, the 56-file timer sweep **wedged the harness after 78 minutes** and could not finish even 14 of those files in ten minutes, where the fixed build does the same 14 in **about one minute at 586/588**. A great many of this engine's hangs were coming from here. **#462 the byte stream** (22/248 → **473/474**, every file 100% on the first build): a byte stream lets the READER supply the buffer — the only way to read a large download without allocating per chunk, which on a hand-me-down laptop is the difference between a file that downloads and a tab the OS kills. A buffer crossing the boundary is **transferred, not shared** (the caller's view is detached on purpose, or every byte in it is a race); a pull-into is satisfied only at an **element boundary**, and the remainder is carried into the next request rather than dropped. `idlharness` 1/2 TIMEOUT → **227/228, one ahead of Chrome**. **CAPS: `streams/transferable/` 0/76 needs stream TRANSFER over `postMessage`** — structured-clone transferables plus separate realms — and is the realm's entire remaining gap to Chrome; **`queuing-strategies-size-function-per-global` 0/2 because iframes share ONE JS realm** (verified: `iframe.contentWindow.CountQueuingStrategy === window.CountQueuingStrategy`); `readable-streams/patched-global` is an instrumentation artefact, probed directly and correct. **⭐ NEXT — `Response.clone()` must TEE the body, not copy its bytes (MEASURED on the finished build, not guessed): `response-clone` is STILL 6/21.** The previous session's read was only half right. `clone()` does `_makeResponse(st.body.bytes.slice())` — so a Response built from a `ReadableStream` clones to NOTHING (`_fetchExtractBody` stores such a body as `{bytes: <empty>, stream}`), and the nine *use structuredClone for teed ReadableStreams* rows fail `assert_false: expected false got true` because the two halves must hand out DIFFERENT chunk objects — a shared buffer lets one reader observe or corrupt the other's bytes. Spec `clone()` tees `this.body` with **`cloneForBranch2 = true`**, and that path now exists (`ReadableStreamDefaultTee` takes the flag and structured-clones branch 2). Small and precise, well under a quest. Scroll: `tickets/456-the-streaming-verdict.md`)

Branch: `engine-per-page-threads`. Previously: 2026-08-03 (Quests #457–#459 — **the data layer arc**: *a page whose `fetch()` fails does not render badly, it renders **empty***. Everything a modern page shows arrives through three objects — `Headers`, `Request`, `Response` — and Obscura had all three as **stubs wearing the name of a class**: a `{}` map and two bags of writable fields. **The `fetch` object-model band 130/566 (23.0%) → 527/598 (88.1%), 27 of 32 files to 100%, two more at exact Chrome parity, ZERO regressions.** Took the frontier survey's own **#1 recommendation** (`fetch` + `streams`, *"the largest winnable block on the map"*) — the previous session took its #3. **The baseline had ZERO could-not-run**, which is the cheapest shape a big failing realm can have: nothing was missing, everything was wrong, and the fix is *build the class properly*. **#457 the header list.** `Headers` is not a map — it is an ordered LIST in which two entries may share a name, which `Set-Cookie` routinely does. Stored as `{name: value}`, **`append()` OVERWROTE**: two cookies arrived and one survived. `get()` must COMBINE matching values with `", "`; iteration is **sorted**, and combined — **except `set-cookie`, which yields one entry per value**, because two cookies glued together are no longer two cookies. The iterator holds a **LIVE index** (delete mid-loop skips an entry, prepend repeats the current one — four tests, and only a live index gets all four). Guards refuse **silently**, not by throwing. **The `no-cors` safelist is checked against what the header WOULD BECOME**, not what you appended — a second harmless value can push the combined pair past 128 bytes. All eight `headers-*` files to 100% on the first build (31/102 → 102/102) plus `header-setcookie` 24/24, never measurable before because `getSetCookie` did not exist. **Rust half: a header value is a ByteString** — values went out UTF-8-encoded (three bytes, not four), and response values were **VANISHING** because `to_str()` accepts only visible ASCII and we mapped the rest to `""`. **#458 the Request.** Every attribute became a read-only accessor on the PROTOTYPE — a request that can be mutated after construction can be **re-aimed after it was vetted**. Every init the engine cannot honour is now refused AT CONSTRUCTION: a URL carrying credentials, `mode:"navigate"` (a mode the *browser* assigns), a non-null `window`, `CONNECT`/`TRACE`/`TRACK`, and **`no-cors` with any method but GET/HEAD/POST** — the three a plain form could already have sent. The guard is applied **before the fill**, so a Request *drops* the `Host` its author tried to send. **And 83 blocked ports: `request-bad-port` was 0/83 with the whole file TIMEOUT** — we opened a connection to port 25, 6667, 2049 and waited, so one `fetch()` could wedge a tab. Fetch's port list is not trivia: every entry is a protocol a shaped HTTP request can DRIVE (SMTP, IMAP, IRC, SSH, NFS), and a browser without it is a cross-protocol attack proxy. Fixed in Rust so it covers XHR and subresources too — **0/83 TIMEOUT → 83/83, hang gone.** **#459 the Response and the body.** Refusals now distinguish WHICH KIND of wrong: a status outside 200–599 is a **RangeError**, a reason phrase with a newline is a **TypeError**. `Response.error()` has immutable headers so script cannot dress a failure up as a success; a network response goes through an **internal** constructor because a response off the wire was not built by script and is not script's to validate (its status may legitimately be 0). **The Body mixin's one rule: a body may be read ONCE**, and `bodyUsed` is not bookkeeping — a second reader would find nothing there. **"Unusable" is BODY-AWARE**: a request with no body can be read and cloned any number of times, and getting that wrong makes an empty response look spent. Constructing a Request from a Request **consumes the input's body** (the input keeps its stream object — callers compare identity — but is spent). **`text()` is "UTF-8 decode", which strips a BOM**, and the same row exposed the `data:` URL processor running on the RAW string instead of a parsed URL, so a non-ASCII char reached the percent-decoder as a code UNIT and came out one mangled byte. **THE SWEEP EARNED ITS KEEP: four XHR rows collapsed on one line** — XHR reads raw response bytes through `resp._bodyBytes`, a field the old stub happened to expose. A rewrite of a shared class breaks whoever reached into its internals, and **the ledger's recorded value is what makes that visible** — 1/10 does not look wrong until it sits next to a row that says 10/10. **CAPS: 54 rows in `header-values*` are blocked in the TRANSPORT, not the engine** — verified by hand over `openssl s_client` that the server accepts and echoes the byte; `hyper`'s HTTP/1 response parser refuses a C0 control in a header value. Enabling reqwest's `http2` feature was tried, changed nothing (ALPN still lands on h1), and was reverted. `*-consume-empty` 13/14 **matches Chrome exactly** on the same file. **⭐ NEXT: `streams` — `response-clone` 6/21 is not a Response bug.** Fifteen rows need a real `ReadableStream`, whose `tee()` currently returns two EMPTY streams; the survey has `streams` at 13.2% vs Chrome 99.5% and named it half of quest F1 *precisely because it sits under `response.body`*. Scroll: `tickets/455-the-data-layer-verdict.md`)

Branch: `engine-per-page-threads`. Previously: 2026-08-02 (Quests #454–#456 — **the accessible arc**: *what is this, and what is it called?* The two questions a screen reader asks to speak a page aloud, and the two an AI agent asks to find the button it was told to press. **The engine could answer neither**, and the whole `accname` realm — the part of WPT that asks exactly those questions — scored **0.0%**, the only realm on the frontier survey at exactly zero, against a Chrome at 98.6% on the same files. **`accname` + `wai-aria/role` 0/853 → 818/853 (95.9%), 31 of 34 files to 100%, ZERO regressions.** Took the standing order over the ⭐: the pointer said `border-width.html` (120/136) and the survey said a realm at zero that is *the product's own mission*. **The 853 zeros were not 853 defects — they were TWO missing primitives**, `computedRole` and `computedLabel`, behind every row (`TypeError: window.test_driver_internal.get_computed_label is not a function`). Now real engine capability exposed as **`Element.computedRole` / `Element.computedLabel`** (the AOM pair, and what WebDriver's Get Computed Role/Label return), so the harness bridge is a **two-line adapter** rather than an implementation. **#454 the computed role.** Obscura already *reflected* `role` — it could store the attribute and hand it back, which is a different question. `role` is a token LIST and abstract roles are not roles (`role="widget"` is not a widget); roles have synonyms (`presentation`≡`none`, `directory`→`list`); and without the attribute the role comes from HTML-AAM, which is not a tag→role table — `<a>` is a link only WITH an href, `<th>` is a columnheader or rowheader by `scope`, `<select>` is a combobox until `multiple`/`size>1`, and **`<img alt="">` is explicitly NOT an image**, it is `none`, unless the author also named it. `role="none"` is a REQUEST the UA must refuse when honouring it would strand the user — and **`tabindex="-1"` counts as focusable** (not tab-reachable, but reachable), plus the presentational-children rule: a list that is `none` has no listitems in it whatever the `<li>` tags say. **#455 the accessible name.** Nine ordered steps, several recursing back into the whole computation. **"Blank" means blank of ASCII whitespace ONLY** — a non-breaking space and the blank braille pattern U+2800 are CONTENT, and an engine that trims Unicode whitespace silently renames the button. **A hidden node contributes nothing UNLESS the computation was pointed at it** — authors park labels in `display:none` spans — but the exemption belongs to the node that was REFERENCED and to what it was hiding, not to a hidden node under a *visible* referent. **And the role and the name are mutually recursive**: ARIA says an unnamed `region`/`form` is not that role at all, so `<nav role="region">` is a navigation and `<div role="ReGiOn group">` falls through to `group` — cut by asking the narrower question the rule turns on, *did the author name it*. **#456 the corners.** `hidden` had to be read as an ATTRIBUTE, not a style (no UA stylesheet, so computed `display` never says `none` on its account). **Two kinds of hidden behave oppositely**: `display:none`/`aria-hidden`/`hidden` remove a subtree, `visibility:hidden` INHERITS and a descendant may set `visible` and reappear — so it must not stop the descent, and because the property inherits, asking each **text node's own parent** is the whole check. A descendant that contributed nothing but SPACE still contributed the space. Each node contributes ONCE and the visited set is **never unwound** (the rule is "already spoken", not "no cycles"). An element may name ITSELF. **`title` beats `placeholder`** (HTML-AAM orders it below the tooltip — 14/22 → 22/22 on one line). `aria-valuetext` beats `aria-valuenow`. And **`aria-owns` RELOCATES** — without it owned content is read TWICE — where `aria-hidden` inherits down the *accessibility* tree (a reparented node escapes it) but `hidden`/`display:none` is a fact about *rendering* that `aria-owns` cannot undo; one predicate for both went 8/9 → **7/9**, two predicates went 9/9. **CAPS: `role_none_conflict_resolution.tentative.html` 0/5 is unwinnable by construction** — every `data-expectedrole` is the literal sentinel `SPEC_AMBIGUOUS_LOG_VALUE`; Chrome fails them too. **27 of the 30 remaining rows need `::before`/`::after` content** — `getComputedStyle` accepts a pseudo argument and IGNORES it — and **3 need to tell an inline child from a block one, which the engine cannot: `getComputedStyle(el).display` answers `block` for EVERY element in the document.** Scroll: `tickets/454-the-accessible-verdict.md`)

Previously: 2026-08-02 (Quests #451–#453 — **the unsupported arc**: three ways a property gets a value wrong before anyone looks at the value. **+242 measured across the whole 244-file `the-stylepropertymap/properties/` band** (10,051 → **10,293/11,311**), ZERO regressions, **ten more files to 100%** (103 → 113), and **23 files now score above Chrome**. **The session's most reusable finding is not a fix.** The pointer named `logical.html` (1274/1468) as the biggest row left; the first move was to ask what **Chrome** scores on it — **1256/1468**. We were already ahead. So Chrome's per-subtest results were pulled (`wpt.fyi` run API → `raw_results_url`, 319 MB) and diffed by subtest name: **130 rows we fail and Chrome passes** (the work list), 64 where the file argues with the spec, and 148 where we were already ahead. Ten minutes turned "194 failures" into 130 winnable rows and 64 named caps. **#451 a corner the engine could store but could not NAME.** css-logical's four flow-relative corner radii (`border-start-start-radius` & siblings — what a page in `vertical-rl` or RTL writes) were absent from `_GCS_DEFAULTS`, so `d.style.setProperty` stored them and `getComputedStyle` read them back while `CSS.supports` said **false** and the Typed OM threw `Invalid propertyName` on all 52 rows. They join the parse/computed **SET** and deliberately not the expansion **ARRAY**: a property that shares a grammar does not share a shorthand, and `border-radius: 5px` must not touch a flow-relative corner. **#452 a shorthand has no typed value of its own.** 82 rows where `set()` accepted a `CSSUnitValue` on a shorthand. Not because the grammar forbids it — it allows it — but because `border-block-start: 5px` only works as a *string* by ALSO resetting the style and the colour to their initials, and a typed value carries no such intent and does not say which longhand it is for; while `margin` is a `{1,4}` box-edge LIST and one value would have to mean "all four". Also: **a `<line-width>` has no percentage in its grammar**, which had leaked only because `set()` wraps an out-of-range value in `calc()` (CSS Values §range-checking refuses only a *literal*) and a `calc()` is waved through for folding. **Named honestly: `margin.html` pins `margin` SHUT to a length and `logical.html` pins `margin-block` OPEN to one — the suite disagrees with itself; we follow each file and say so, and Chrome refuses both.** **#453 reification asks the PROPERTY too.** `1px` is not a length on `margin`, and handing back a `CSSUnitValue` gives a page something it can do arithmetic on and then not set anywhere. The gate that refuses a value on the way IN is the same question — one table, two doors. Three faces: a shorthand's numeric; **a colour**, because `red`, `#f00` and `rgb(255,0,0)` are one value with three spellings and a `CSSKeywordValue` compares by SPELLING (`currentcolor` is the exception and not an inconsistency — it names no colour, it points at one); and **a pair-valued computed value** — a corner radius computes to two radii that merely *serialize* as one token when they agree. Plus the matching computed-value fix: `_CLAMP_NEG_PROPS` clamped a resolved negative **px** to `0px` and let a negative **percentage** through, so `padding-block-start` computed to `-3.14%`. **CAPS named: the last 24 `logical.html` rows are a SUITE SELF-CONTRADICTION** — `border-radius.html` demands the base `CSSStyleValue` for a corner's computed value and `logical.html` demands a `CSSUnitValue`, for the SAME corner; answering two ways depending on spelling would be indefensible, so we follow the file that reasoned about it. The `currentcolor`-computed rows are a cap **no engine passes** (Chrome fails all four). Scroll: `tickets/101-the-unsupported-verdict.md`)

Previously: 2026-08-02 (Quests #448–#450 — **the well-formed arc**: a zero that lost its unit, eight colours nobody had written, and three rules `class` gets backwards. **+463 measured**, ZERO regressions, **ten files to 100%**, `css/css-typed-om/idlharness.html` 270 → **536/544**. **#448 a unitless zero is a length, and an angle.** `_tomReify` already knew that `width: 0` is `0px` and not `0` — but it asks the PROPERTY which unit a bare zero wore, and `_tomComponentOf` called it with no property at all, because inside a transform function the property has nothing to say: **the FUNCTION does**. So `CSSStyleValue.parse('transform', 'translateX(0)')` threw `Failed to construct 'CSSTranslate': expected a length`, and the same throw came out of `computedStyleMap().get('transform')` on **the single commonest transform on the whole web — the `translateX(0)` GPU-promotion hack**. css-transforms-1 spells out every argument slot and admits `<zero>` in the angle slots as well as the length ones; `_TOM_TF_SLOTS` is that table. It was not a formatting bug — the harness reported those 38 rows as `ReferenceError: skewX is not defined`, i.e. **could-not-run, not failure**, because the test file's whole setup block died on it. **#449 a colour channel is the one place a bare JS number does not mean `CSS.number(n)`.** In a colour `0.5` means HALF, so a `<percentage>` slot reads it as `50%`, an `<angle>` slot reads `180` as `180deg`, and Lab's `<number>` slots read `7` as `7` — and the channel's IDL type also decides **which error a refusal is**: `CSSHSL`'s hue is a union that admits a keyword, so `new CSSHSL(undefined, …).h` is quite legitimately `CSSKeywordValue("undefined")`, while `CSSHWB`'s hue is a plain `CSSNumericValue` and refuses the same argument at the IDL door with a **TypeError** where the union slots let it in and then judge its TYPE with a SyntaxError. Two colours, the same wrong argument, two different errors, and the IDL says so. Eight files 0 → **182, all to 100%**. **#450 three rules `class` gets backwards, and each one is a difference a page can see.** An interface object on the global is **not** enumerable and `globalThis.X = C` makes it so, so every `for (k in window)` swept up the platform. Prototype members **are** enumerable and class methods are not — the exact opposite — so `Object.keys(CSSUnitValue.prototype)` came back empty. And an accessor **brand-checks its receiver**: `get value() { return this._k }` quietly answers `undefined` off the prototype where WebIDL says TypeError, and a silent `undefined` is a bug that surfaces three call-frames later. Plus: a constructor's `length` is its count of REQUIRED arguments and an interface with no constructor has **none at all** (`new CSSStyleValue()` is a TypeError, which is why internal construction goes through `_tomMake`); a WebIDL `iterable<T>`'s `entries`/`values`/`@@iterator` are **the very `Array.prototype` functions**, identity and all; and the twelve missing small/large/dynamic-viewport units (`svi`, `lvmin`, `dvmax`, …) were two thirds of each family. **CAP, named honestly: 8 of the remaining idlharness rows are a harness quirk** — `assert_own_property(CSSUnitValue, 'parse')` for a static declared on `CSSStyleValue`; WebIDL reaches a parent's statics through the interface object's [[Prototype]], never as own properties, so no conformant engine can pass them. Scroll: `tickets/448-the-well-formed-verdict.md`)

Previously: 2026-08-01 (Quests #445–#447 — **the typed-value arc**: a serializer nobody had written, a realm that died on line one, and a `var()` being read as arithmetic. **+10,767 measured**, ZERO regressions, **142 files to 100%**. **#445 the SPECIFIED value keeps the author's own function.** `commitStyles()` freezes an animation into the inline style, and that is the THIRD serialization of one animated transform: `getComputedStyle` shows the resolved matrix, `computedStyleMap()` the computed list (#444), and `div.style.transform` the specified value — `translateX(25px)` where the other two say `matrix(1, 0, 0, 1, 25, 0)` and `translate(25px, 0px)`. The rule is #444's own sentence one layer up: **a pair serializes as what the two sides AGREE on**, so an author's spelling survives only when both endpoints wrote it, and `translateY(0%)` → `translateX(50%)` is a `translate` again. Plus the trailing-default trim, where **`scale`'s second argument defaults to the FIRST** (`scale(1.5, 1.5)` is `scale(1.5)`; `scale(1.5, 2)` is not) and the 3D functions never elide. **And half of it is not a serializer at all: `commitStyles` sets a VALUE, not a STRING** — seven rows came back lowercased (`scalex`, `skewx`) because the commit met the parser on the way in. `transform-valid.html` pinning `skewX(90deg)`→`skewx(90deg)` is not in conflict with that; they are two different doors. `transform-interpolation-inline-value` 24 → **41/41**. **#446 a realm that died on line one.** `css/css-typed-om/` is 359 files and the ~245 under `the-stylepropertymap/properties/` all pull in one shared `testsuite.js` that CONSTRUCTS a `CSSKeywordValue`, a `CSSUnitValue`, a `CSSMathSum`, a `CSSUnparsedValue` and a twelve-component `CSSTransformValue` **at script load** — so every one of them died with `ReferenceError: CSSKeywordValue is not defined` before a single test ran. #430's shape one realm over. Built the value classes for real: a numeric-type algebra where `3px*3px` is `{length: 2}` and a bare percentage has no type until it meets something; the 34-unit table with conversion factors, where the relative lengths have **no** factor because they cannot be converted without an element and a viewport; the seven math classes; `CSSUnparsedValue`; the eight transform components, whose `toMatrix()` re-enters this engine's own transform parser rather than growing a second builder that could disagree with it; reification, normalization, `attributeStyleMap`, `rule.styleMap` and the `CSS.px()` factories. **#447 two answers again, and a `var()` read as arithmetic.** The `set()` gate asked `CSS.supports`, which says yes to `width: -3.14%` while `style.width = '-3.14%'` silently keeps the old value — #442's arc exactly; it now asks a scratch declaration, the same parser the real write will meet. Out of RANGE is not the same as invalid (`width: calc(-3.14%)` IS a width, which is why the suite wants a `CSSMathSum` back out of a `CSSUnitValue`), and a full per-property grammar is not the answer — **inverting the question is**: ask which properties accept an ANGLE at all and the list is short and closed. **The bug the probe found is not a Typed OM bug: `opacity: var(--A)` came back out as `var(-1 * (-1 * a))`** — `_FILTER_MATH_RE` counts `var(` as a math function on purpose (for VALIDITY an unsubstituted reference must be accepted), but the callers using it to decide *fold this* handed `--A` to the calc parser, which read the two dashes as two unary minuses. The computed value came back `1`, the initial — so `opacity: var(--fade)` did not merely serialize oddly, **it did not work**. `css/css-typed-om/**` 65 → **10,815**. **Ten ledger rows in the calc/colour band flagged as regressions were STALE and all ten measured byte-identical on the pre-session build — corrected in place below.** Scroll: `tickets/445-the-typed-verdict.md`)

Previously: 2026-08-01 (Quests #442–#444 — **the two-answers arc**: three places where the engine gave two different answers to the same question. **+110 measured**, ZERO regressions, **two files to 100%** (`transform-interpolation-computed-value` 0 → **82/82**, `transform-interpolation-006` 76 → **96/96**). **#442 there were TWO declaration parsers and only one of them validated.** `_parseStyleDecls` (CSSOM) runs the whole per-property validity chain; `_cssParseDecls` — the cascade-shape parser `getComputedStyle` reads through `_buildCascade`/`_cascadeResolve` — validated custom properties and nothing else. So `.p { transform: translate(30px) } .p { transform: 30px }` rendered with **no** translation: the garbage won the cascade and displaced the good declaration it should have lost to. Four lines route one through the other; it is the widest shared change this file has taken all campaign. **The one subtest it broke was worth more than the subtest**: `el.style` and `style=""` are one declaration block with two faces, nothing writes back from the live one to the attribute (`_styleWriteback` is gated on custom elements), and `_buildCascade` was reading both — so `div.style.animation = ''` cancelled the animation in one home and left it running in the other. It had been invisible only because the lax parser mangled the attribute's copy into something that never ran: two bugs cancelling. **#443 a declaration ends at a TOP-LEVEL semicolon.** Both parsers did `String(text).split(';')`, which tears `style="background-image:url(data:image/svg+xml;base64,…)"` in half — an unclosed `url()` plus a second half that still has a colon in it and so parses as a declaration for a property named `base64,PHN2Zy8+)`. The image never loads and nothing else looks wrong. **#444 each family has a FLAT primitive and a DEEP one, and the function picks.** `scaleZ(1)` → `scaleZ(2)` is `scale3d(1, 1, 1.5)`, not `scalez(1.5)` — a function that names the z axis has already left the plane, even when nothing on the other side does — and a flat function meeting its deep sibling is not the mismatch that ends the walk (#441). Plain 2D `rotate` is the one rotation that keeps its own spelling; `skewX`/`skewY` are primitives in their own right. Reachable at all only through **`computedStyleMap()`**, the one API that reports the computed LIST rather than the resolved matrix — there are now three serializations of one animated transform and they are three different values. Scroll: `tickets/442-the-reconciled-verdict.md`)

Previously: 2026-08-01 (Quests #439–#441 — **the matrix-decomposition arc**: the whole remaining tail of the transform realm really was one algorithm. **+394 measured across 7 files**, ZERO regressions, **SIX files to 100%** (`transform-interpolation-005` 204 → **384/384**, `transform-matrix-composition` 36 → **112/112**, `transform-interpolation-004` 216 → **288/288**, `list-interpolation` 44 → **76/76**, `transform-interpolation-003` 150 → **168/168**, `transform-composition` 46 → **56/56**). **#439**: two matrices with the same name and the same six arguments walked into the generic skeleton kit, which averaged the entries one by one — but `matrix(1, 0, 0, 1, 0, -6)` → `matrix(0, 7, -1, 0, 6, 0)` **is** a quarter turn with a sevenfold stretch, and averaging is not a slower version of that, it is a different motion. So each matrix is decomposed into `translate · rotate · skew · scale` (and in 3D a perspective and a quaternion), those components interpolate, and the result is recomposed. 2D and 3D are two different algorithms, not one with the z terms dropped. **The 2D y-scale is the DETERMINANT over the x-scale — which is how the sign of a reflection lands in the scale rather than in the rotation** — and a zero angle stands in as 360° before "don't turn the long way round" is applied. **The trap: the 3D recomposition's rotation matrix is the TRANSPOSE of the one the spec's pseudo-code appears to write**, because the decomposition negates three quaternion components to fix a sign convention; get it wrong and every 3D answer comes back mirrored, which looks exactly like an arithmetic bug. **#440 a matrix that cannot be inverted has no decomposition**, and the spec's fallback is DISCRETE — `matrix(1, 1, 0, 0, 0, 100)` folds the plane onto a line, and its expectations *step* at 0.5 rather than sliding. **The discreteness belongs to the WHOLE LIST, not to the one function**: the singular matrix is identical on both sides, so a per-function view would cheerfully interpolate the translation beside it. That null had to reach the transitions predicate too — so `_waInterpolable` now **asks the interpolation itself** at a `t` that cannot matter, one entry point, and #429's rule holds for free. Matrix accumulation is entry-wise about the identity: m11/m22/m33/m44 accumulate as `a + b − 1`. **#441 a mismatch ENDS the walk; it does not condemn the list** — the spec post-multiplies only the **REMAINING** functions of each side into two matrices. Which is worth 32 subtests in one file: `rotate(0deg) translate(100px)` → `rotate(720deg) scale(2) translate(200px)` mismatches at position 1, so the rotation ahead of it still winds through two entire turns and reads `rotate(180deg)` a quarter of the way in. Flatten the lists whole and that becomes a quarter of nothing at all — **a matrix is where a turn count goes to die**. Scroll: `tickets/439-the-decomposed-verdict.md`)

Previously: 2026-08-01 (Quests #436–#438 — **the transform-list arc**: the engine was animating a value the spec never asks it to animate, and everything else followed from that. **+446 measured across 13 files**, ZERO regressions, **five files to 100%** (`transform-interpolation-001` 256 → **448/448**, `transform-interpolation-002` 186 → **216/216**, `transform-scale-composition` 56 → **98/98**, `transform-perspective-composition` 8 → **28/28**, `transform-rotate-composition` 114 → **126/126**). Bucketing `transform-interpolation-001`'s 283 failing rows split them by HARNESS, not by geometry: eleven of nineteen value pairs failed on both CSS Transitions harnesses and **passed on Web Animations** — the same endpoints answered two ways, which is never a geometry bug. **#436**: `getComputedStyle(el).transform` reports a matrix, and that is the **resolved** value; css-transforms-1 says the **computed** value is the specified list *with lengths made absolute* — still a list. Every animation-model intake (transition endpoints, the underlying value, the style-change snapshot) read through CSSOM and so got the matrix, and a matrix has forgotten which functions made it: `rotate(30deg)` → `rotate(330deg)` took the 60° short way instead of the 300° long way, and `scaleX(2)` accumulating `scaleX(3)` answered **6** because a failed alignment falls back to concatenation and concatenating transforms MULTIPLIES them. Fixed with one serialization mode + a latch, so the value still comes down the ordinary cascade. **The trap that cost a build: the re-entrancy guard has to be in force for the WHOLE computation** — resolving a keyframe's `2em` asks the cascade for font-size, which walks back into the very animation being computed. **#437 `perspective` interpolates its RECIPROCAL** — halfway from 400px to 500px is **421.05px**, because what moves evenly is the matrix entry −1/d, not the distance to the eye. Which makes `perspective(none)` an ordinary endpoint (depth ∞, reciprocal 0) and therefore the function's **identity**, so a list one perspective short can be padded after all. **#438 every rotation is an AXIS and an ANGLE** — and unlike translate/scale/skew the family does not reduce by padding, because an axis is a direction, not a magnitude. Promoted to `rotate3d` and handed to the rules the `rotate` PROPERTY already lives by: this quest is thirteen lines calling #435's kit unchanged. Scroll: `tickets/436-the-listed-verdict.md`)

Previously: 2026-07-31 (Quests #433–#435 — **the individual-transform arc**: `translate` / `scale` / `rotate` were parsed, computed and serialized by three real grammars, and then handed to the **generic slot model**, which had never been told what any of them MEAN. **+852 measured across 11 files**, ZERO regressions, **six files 0-ish → 100%** (`scale-composition` 8→**80/80**, `translate-composition` 10→**112/112**, `rotate-composition` 54→**132/132**, `scale-interpolation` 154→**360/360**, `translate-interpolation` 228→**408/408**, `rotate-interpolation` 164→**360/360**). The two files the last knight flagged as "did not move AT ALL" while every neighbour did were not a composition bug at all — they were the whole property type missing. **#433 `scale`**: a missing component is a DEFAULT, not a mismatch (y defaults to x, z to 1), so `1` and `10 -5 0` are the same shape once written out — and **adding two scalings MULTIPLIES them** (2 ⊕ 3 = 6, not 5), because adding transforms means COMPOSING them. **#434 `translate`**: three `<length-percentage>`s padded from `0px`, and the two numbers of a computed length-percentage cannot say whether a percentage is *involved* — `calc(0% + 480px)` and `480px` hold the same pair and serialize differently, so the flag rides along and ORs across the pair. **#435 `rotate`**: an axis and an angle, not four numbers. Same line → the angle adds and slides (which is the only reason `100deg` → `-100deg` passes through 0 and `900deg` means anything); different lines → SLERP, with **both quaternions put in the w ≥ 0 half of the sphere FIRST** — skip that and a full turn reads as w = −1 and sends the interpolation the long way round (295° where 25° was wanted). And the platform serializes with **six SIGNIFICANT digits, not six decimal places**: `124.97530385…` in full rounds to 124.98 at the precision the tests compare at, cut to six figures it is `124.975`, which rounds to 124.97. Scroll: `tickets/433-the-individual-verdict.md`)

Previously: 2026-07-31 (Quests #430–#432 — **the registration arc**: a property the engine has never heard of does not fail a test, it fails **every** test in the file on the first line — `interpolation-testcommon.js` opens each subtest with `assert_true(CSS.supports(property, to))`. **+1112 measured across 35 files**, ZERO regressions, **fifteen files to 100%**. **#430 `accent-color`** — `auto | <color>`, inherits; it lives in BOTH `_CSSUI_VALIDATED` (the grammar gate; the generic colour gate validates nothing) and `_COLOR_PROPS` (the computed dispatch), and `auto` is a **keyword, not a colour** — short-circuited to itself ahead of the colour path via `_COLOR_AUTO_PROPS`, or the model would happily interpolate a keyword against `green`. **#431 `aspect-ratio`** — `auto || <ratio>`; the generic slot model would have found a perfectly good skeleton (`<num> / <num>`) and quietly done the WRONG arithmetic in it, because css-values-4 §combine-ratio interpolates **the LOGARITHMS** — which is what makes the midpoint of 1:2 and 2:1 be `1 / 1` and not `1.25 / 1`. A degenerate ratio (either number 0 or infinite) and disagreeing `auto` keywords have no logarithm and are honestly discrete. `<ratio>` also defines **no addition**: `Vresult = Va`, and Va is the *underlying* value. **Chasing its last two subtests found the session's biggest win: `animation-composition` was parsed, computed, reflected — and never READ.** Declared inside a keyframe block it is that keyframe's own composite operation, but it sits in `_CS_NEVER` and `_caKeyframesOf` consulted `_CS_NEVER` one line too early, so every `add`/`accumulate` keyframe silently replaced. **The whole `*-composition.html` band went 989 → 1591 (+602) across 29 files, twelve to 100%.** **#432 `object-view-box`** — `none | <basic-shape-rect>`, a thin wrapper over the shared `_opShape` engine with one deliberate difference: `clip-path`/`offset-path` rewrite `rect()`/`xywh()` into `inset()` at computed time and this does NOT. The rewrite is affine so the tests would have passed either way — which is exactly why it is worth naming; expectations that round-trip through our own `getComputedStyle` cannot catch a serialization that is merely self-consistent. **Correction: the ⭐ named four properties; `contrast-color-interpolation.html` is a REFTEST with no testharness — three, not four.** Scroll: `tickets/430-the-registered-verdict.md`)

Previously: 2026-07-31 (Quests #427–#429 — **the interpolation-endpoints arc**: the whole `interpolation-testcommon.js` family swept to its edges across `css-sizing/`, `css-ui/`, `css-flexbox/`, `css-grid/`, `css-shapes/`, `css-color/`, `css-images/`, `css-multicol/`, `filter-effects/` and `css-transforms/` — **+2448 measured across 83 files**, ZERO regressions, **sixteen files to 100%**. #424's pointer said the ~110 remaining files needed no new engine work; they didn't — they needed **three primitives in the shared interpolation core**, each costing every file in every directory a fixed fraction of its subtests. **#427 a progress outside [0,1] EXTRAPOLATES** — `interpolation-testcommon.js` samples `at (-0.3)` and `at (1.5)` with a deliberately overshooting `cubic-bezier`, and the timing model already produced those values; the interval lookup then threw them away twice, by falling off the end and by clamping the interval distance. web-animations-1 §effect-value takes the last keyframe whose offset is ≤ progress **and < 1** (or, when progress is negative, the last at offset 0) and **the next keyframe BY INDEX**, and does not clamp the distance — so the first/last interval is EXTENDED. Plus: a UA-supplied `from`/`to` keyframe carries the animation's `animation-timing-function`, not linear. **#428 a CSS-wide keyword is a question for the CASCADE** — `_waComputedValue` handed `initial` to `_normComputed`, which normalises a *value*; the keyword survived as a string, no length parsed out of it, and every `from [initial]/[inherit]/[unset]` row STEPPED instead of sliding. Now resolved through the cascade (initial value / parent's computed / the property's inheritance), ahead of the `_WA_UNCOMPUTED` escape so `transform: initial` becomes `none`. The same keyword one layer lower: **`_isValidFilter` lacked the two escapes `_isValidTransform` already had**, so `filter: initial` was rejected by the style setter and never became a declaration at all — a real-page bug. **#429 a pair that cannot be INTERPOLATED is not a transition** — css-transitions-2 §transition-behavior: under the default `normal` a non-interpolable pair starts NO transition, so `max-width: auto → 20px` shows `20px` from the first frame instead of jumping at the halfway mark. `globalThis._waInterpolable` is `_waInterpolate`'s own decision hoisted ahead of the fact. `visibility` is the exception that proves it (its own animation type, not discrete) — gating it out cost `properties-value-001` two subtests and its `transitionend`, which is what the held-realm sweep is for. Making the model observable also punished a gap in it, so the filter list got #425's transform treatment: **`none` IS the empty list**, pad at the tail with each function's **lacuna value**, and #417's skeleton kit does the arithmetic. Scroll: `tickets/427-the-extrapolated-verdict.md`)

| `css/css-transitions/properties-value-001.html` | 0/560 | **560/560** | ✅ 100% | **Quest #421 The Transitioned Verdict.** The single widest untouched tail on the board. `transition-*` parsed perfectly and never ran: `wpt_fails` said `must not be target value after start` and `expected "background-color:2s" but got ""`, 560 times. **A CSS transition IS an `Animation` with a two-keyframe effect** — so it needed no value machinery of its own, only a style change event, a `CSSTransition` object, and a cascade seat above everything else. The style change event is a **forced flush**: Obscura has no rendering loop, so `getComputedStyle` / `offset*` / `getAnimations()` / DOMContentLoaded / the next task boundary after a mutation is the whole mechanism — and that is exactly where the difference becomes observable. Gated four deep so a page that never transitions pays one integer compare: a generation counter (`_styleGen` + `_cssomInlineGen`), a per-`<style>`-text cache of the declaring selectors, ONE `querySelectorAll` over their union, and a per-element fingerprint (own + ancestors' id/class/style, inline-CSSOM write counter, tree generation) that skips every candidate whose style could not have moved — two elements out of fifty per flush here. **+560.** |

The same expansion decides `transition: padding-left .01s, padding .02s` in favour of `.02s`: the last matching layer wins, and a layer matches through its shorthand. **+9.** |
| `css/css-transitions/events-002.html` · `-003` · `-004` · `-005` · `-008` | 0/2 · 0/1 · 0/2 · 0/3 · 0/3 | **2/2 · 1/1 · 2/2 · 3/3 · 3/3** | ✅ 100% | **Quest #422.** The four-event lifecycle: `transitionrun` at creation (queued, not inline — a transition is created in the middle of a `getComputedStyle` and a listener must not re-enter the cascade from inside the flush that made it), `transitionstart` at the active phase, then exactly one of `transitionend`/`transitioncancel`. `elapsedTime` measures the ACTIVE interval, so run/start report `min(max(-delay,0),duration)`: a negative delay is elapsed time, not delay (`-003`). `-002`: a property with no previous declaration transitions from its inherited-or-initial value. `-004`: a transition declared purely through the CSSOM never reaches the style attribute, and declares itself *and* moves the value inside ONE style change event — so scripted-inline elements are snapshotted from their first write. `-008`: `TransitionEvent.animation` as a real prototype attribute. **+11.** |

One hook, +53 subtests across eight files. Plus: `transition-timing-function` is the EFFECT's easing, not a keyframe's. **+22.** |

| `css/css-transitions/CSSTransition-effect.tentative.html` · `-currentTime` · `-startTime` · `-ready` · `-finished` · `-transitionProperty` | 0/10 · 0/5 · 0/5 · 0/2 · 0/1 · 0/1 | **8/10 · 4/5 · 3/5 · 2/2 · 1/1 · 1/1** | 🟢 79.2% | **Quest #423.** The **reversing shortening factor**: sending a half-faded element back where it came from must not take a full duration — the factor is `abs(current value fraction × old factor + (1 − old factor))`, it **compounds**, and the new transition's reversing-adjusted start value is the old one's END value. | `css/css-transitions/transitioncancel-001.html` · `transitions-retarget` · `changing-while-transition-001–004` · `before-load-001` · `starting-of-transitions-001` · `transition-important` · `transition-zero-duration-with-delay` · `transitioncancel-002/003` | 0 each | **1/1 each** | ✅ 100% | **Quests #422–#423.** `transitioncancel-001` sets a class on `load` with no prior `getComputedStyle` anywhere — so nothing had ever recorded a before-change style and the first transition was swallowed. A browser has computed style by DOMContentLoaded; now so has Obscura, and `before-load-001`, `changing-while-transition-001..004`, `starting-of-transitions-001` and `transition-important` came in behind it. `transitions-retarget` forces its recalc with `offsetTop`, so all four `offset*` metrics flush, not just width/height. `transition-important` is the cascade seat: transitions out-rank an author `!important`. **+12.** |
| `web-animations/interfaces/Animation/style-change-events.html` | 23/25 | **24/25** | 🟢 96.0% | **Quest #423 (caught by the zero-regression sweep).** Fell to 3/25 with *"A transition should NOT have been triggered"*, and the test was right: §starting-of-transitions says the before-change style is the previous recalc's computed style **"except with any styles derived from declarative animations … updated to the current time"**. A property an animation is currently supplying therefore has the SAME before- and after-change value — the animation clobbers both — and can never start a transition, however far the underlying specified value moved. Reading the stale snapshot was the bug; asking `_waAnimatedDecls` for the element's current animation-only declarations and skipping any property it owns fixed it, and left the file one subtest ABOVE its baseline. **+1.** |
| `web-animations/animation-model/` (24-file suite) | 1817/2352 | **2157/2352** | 🟢 91.7% | **Quests #418–#420 The Typed Verdict.** #417's ⭐ named next-leverage plus the two larger buckets beside it — all three **typing** failures. **#418 the colour as a value slot**: `#ff0000` and `rgb(255, 0, 0)` had nothing in common under a numbers-and-literals model, so colour addition fell through to replace. A colour is ONE token — a second kind of hole in the same skeleton makes every spelling the same shape, gives it premultiplied arithmetic, and makes colour PAIRS (`scrollbar-color`) and colour+length values (`box-shadow`) work with no pair-specific code. **Clamp once, at the end** — 128 + 255 = 383 has to survive interpolation, or the midpoint reads 192 instead of 255. **#419 `rem` against the root font-size**: `rem` was a constant 16; it needs no per-element context, so it resolves inside `_evalMath` with zero call-site changes — with a latch saying that a `rem` in the ROOT's own font-size means the INITIAL font-size. **#420 keyframe values are computed values**: §keyframes says so, and skipping it is why `1rem` onto `10px` answered `1rem`. Three honest exceptions — `opacity` (the spec clamps the RESULT, not the endpoints), the transform family (resolved value is a matrix, computed value is the LIST that animates), and `var()` (substituted where the declaration resolves) — the last two caught as live regressions and fixed. Plus **a length-percentage is two components**, so `10%` onto `10px` is `calc(10% + 10px)`. New `_installClickFocus()` — a trusted, non-canceled `mousedown` listener that focuses the nearest `_isFocusableArea` ancestor of the press target. Fires before pointerup (light dismiss) and click (invoker activation), so a click on an invoker focuses the button then the hide/dismiss runs; if the pressed control sat inside the closing popover, `_restorePreviousFocus` wins (it now `display:none` → unfocusable). Trusted-gated so the scripted `.click()` METHOD (untrusted, no mousedown) never shifts focus — the passing "Popover focus test" family relies on that. **+10, ZERO regressions.** |

| `domparsing/insert_adjacent_html.html` | 2/31 | **31/31** | ✅ 100% | **Quest #126 The Adjacent Verdict.** Rewrote `Element.insertAdjacentHTML` (`bootstrap.js`) to the real spec algorithm. Now: ASCII case-insensitive position; resolves the insertion **context** and throws BEFORE parsing — unknown position → `SyntaxError`, `beforebegin`/`afterend` with no parent or a Document parent → `NoModificationAllowedError` (code 7); parses in a context-named throwaway element (`<html>`→`<body>`), flags parsed `<script>`s "already started" (`_scriptAlreadyStarted`, the Quest #125 inertness flag) so they never execute on insertion, then inserts the parsed nodes via a **DocumentFragment** atomically (no live-NodeList hazard, no text-node merging). **+29.** |

Now the page document (and standalone `new Document()`) clone into a fresh **detached** document of the same kind (HTML `DetachedDocument` / XML `new Document()`), deep-cloning children into the clone. **Root-cause #2:** a cloned `<script>` now carries the "already started" flag (`_scriptAlreadyStarted`) so `appendChild`'s inline-script eval skips it — without this, deep-cloning a page that contains its own `<script>` re-executed it and recursed to OOM. | `dom/nodes/Document-constructor.html` | 3/5 | **5/5** | ✅ 100% | **Quest #40 The Standalone Charter.** The `new Document()` web ctor (named "next leverage" since #34). It returned a `DetachedDocument` subclass → `Object.getPrototypeOf(doc) !== Document.prototype`, and its XML `createElement` used `_wrapEl` (HTML interface) → `createElement("a").constructor !== Element`. Now `new Document()` is a genuine `Document` instance set up as **standalone** (real fragment backing node + `_standalone`/`_kind:'xml'`/`_createMode:'xml'`); base getters branch on `_standalone` for application/xml content type, about:blank URL, self-scoped queries, non-HTML createElement (case-preserving plain `Element` via shared `_createElementXMLInto`), `_ownerDoc`-tagging factories, and a working `createCDATASection`. Caught & fixed a latent regression — `dom/common.js` does `new Document().createCDATASection(...)`, and the base throwing version aborted the whole harness (TreeWalker/Range went dark). Pure JS, no new Rust. **+2** |
| `dom/nodes/DOMImplementation-createDocumentType.html` | 1/82 | **82/82** | ✅ 100% | **Quest #39 The Doctype Charter.** Exposed by #38. `createDocumentType` had no "valid doctype name" check and the wrong `ownerDocument`. Per DOM §createDocumentType a name is valid unless it contains ASCII whitespace / U+0000 / `>` (the empty string is valid — *looser* than createElementNS's QName, so `:foo`/`foo:`/`prefix::local`/`@` are fine); only `"edi:>"`/`"edi:a "` throw `InvalidCharacterError`. The doctype's node document must be the implementation's associated document — but `DetachedDocument`/`_IframeDocument` overrode `get implementation()` to return the **page's** impl, so `doc.implementation.createDocumentType(...)` owned to the page. Fix: `Document.get implementation()` captures `this` and sets the doctype's `_ownerDoc`; the delegating overrides removed (inherit the bound getter); `<3 args` → `TypeError`. Pure JS, no new Rust. **+81** |
| `dom/nodes/DOMImplementation-createDocument.html` | 320/434 | **434/434** | ✅ 100% | **Quest #38 The Document Charter.** The widest DOM frontier left (the `new Document()` footgun named since #34). 114 fails were all document-identity `assert_equals` (distinct objects, not `===`). `XMLDocument` was `class extends Document {}` (abstract, no backing node) and `createDocument` returned a `DetachedDocument`, so `Object.getPrototypeOf(doc) === XMLDocument.prototype` never held. Now `XMLDocument extends DetachedDocument` (a real fragment-backed node — distinct backing Document, no node-0 fallback) and `createDocument` returns `new XMLDocument('xml')` + full WebIDL: `<2 args`/bad-doctype → `TypeError`, `namespace` `DOMString?`, `qualifiedName` `[LegacyNullToEmptyString]`, document element created BEFORE the doctype is appended. DOMParser's XML branch still returns a sibling `_IframeDocument` so `!(doc instanceof XMLDocument)` holds for parsed docs. **+114** |

## Known hard swamps (left as-is — diminishing returns / high risk)

- **url-constructor remaining ~57 fails**: deep `url`-crate-vs-WHATWG divergences —
  ~39 `file:` scheme arcana (Windows drive letters `w|`→`w:`, backslash/slash
  normalization, `file:////` empty host), non-special-path whitespace `%20`
  encoding, and path-only-with-slashes inputs (`<///test>`) the `url` crate rejects.
- A proper fix path would be a more spec-aligned URL parser; chasing these risks
  the 833 that already pass.
